=== NOTES 1:23 | The Sixteen Mahajanapadas – List, Capitals, Monarchies vs Ganasanghas
## SUB 0 | Mahajanapada → Capital → Region
[23:0:0] TABLE head: Mahajanapada | Capital | Region / river
  [23:0:0.0] Anga | Champa | Bhagalpur, Munger (east Bihar); Ganga–Champa confluence
  [23:0:0.1] Magadha | Rajagriha / Girivraja → Pataliputra | Patna, Nalanda, Gaya – the area south of the Ganga
  [23:0:0.2] Kashi | Varanasi | Between the Varuna and Asi
  [23:0:0.3] Kosala | Shravasti, Saket (Ayodhya) | Awadh; Sarayu
  [23:0:0.4] Vajji | Vaishali | North Bihar (Vaishali, Muzaffarpur, Darbhanga)
  [23:0:0.5] Malla | Kushinara, Pava | Kushinagar, Deoria
  [23:0:0.6] Chedi | Sotthivati / Suktimati | Eastern Bundelkhand
  [23:0:0.7] Vatsa | Kaushambi | Prayagraj; Yamuna
  [23:0:0.8] Kuru | Indraprastha (also Hastinapura) | Meerut, Delhi, Thaneshwar
  [23:0:0.9] Panchala | North Ahichchhatra, south Kampilya | Rohilkhand (Bareilly, Badaun, Farrukhabad)
  [23:0:0.10] Matsya | Viratnagar (Bairat) | Jaipur, Alwar, Bharatpur – Rajasthan
  [23:0:0.11] Shurasena | Mathura | Braj; Yamuna
  [23:0:0.12] Assaka / Ashmaka | Potana / Potali = Bodhan | Godavari
  [23:0:0.13] Avanti | North Ujjayini (ancient Avantika), south Mahishmati | Malwa; Shipra, Narmada
  [23:0:0.14] Gandhara | Taxila (Pushkalavati) | Peshawar–Rawalpindi
  [23:0:0.15] Kamboja | Rajapura / Hatak (PMF: Poonch) | J&K, Hazara; Kubha (Kabul); horse-breeding
[23:0:1] - **Not correctly matched (UPRO 2017):** Kosala – Ahichchhatra (Ahichchhatra is **North Panchala**).
[23:0:2] - **APPSC G1 2024:** Avanti–Ujjain, Kosala–Shravasti, Vatsa–Kaushambi, Anga–Champa.
[23:0:3] - **8 of 16** in present Uttar Pradesh; Bihar: Anga, Magadha, Vajji.
[23:0:4] - **Kashi** was ruled by the **Brahmadatta** dynasty until **Kosala** annexed it.
[23:0:5] > Ahichchhatra = Panchala; Suktimati = Chedi; Viratnagar = Matsya; Champa = Anga; Mathura = Shurasena.
[23:0:6] ~~ Sources: Rocket Indian History Part 2 p.110 (Sheet #138); PMF Ancient & Medieval Part 2 p.20-27; NextGen Indian History - APPSC Exams Part 3 p.27; NCERT Class 6 ch.1; old app notes row 23
## SUB 1 | Gana-sanghas – Vajji, Malla, Shakya
[23:1:0] TABLE head: Aspect | Gana-sangha | Monarchy
  [23:1:0.0] Rule | Many rajas in an assembly of clan heads | Single hereditary king
  [23:1:0.1] Hall | Santhagara | Royal court
  [23:1:0.2] Location | NW and Himalayan foothills | Ganga plain
  [23:1:0.3] Examples | Vajji, Malla | Kosala, Magadha, Vatsa
[23:1:1] - **Vajji confederacy:** 8 clans – **Lichchhavis** (most powerful), Videhas, Jnatrikas, Vajjis; capital **Vaishali** = seat of the **world's first republic**; led by **Chetaka**, maternal uncle of **Mahavira**.
[23:1:2] - **Malla:** confederacy of 9 clans; capitals **Kushinara** (Buddha's **Mahaparinirvana**) and **Pava** (Mahavira died). A-R (UPPSC 2004): Kushinagar = Malla capital and parinirvana site, both true, R not the explanation.
[23:1:3] - **Buddha-period republics:** Shakya (Kapilavastu), Bhagga (Sumsumaragiri), Buli (Alakappa), Kalama (Kesaputta), Koliya (Ramagama), Malla, Lichchhavi. **Yaudheya** is NOT one – it is a post-Mauryan republic of Punjab–Haryana.
[23:1:4] - **Kamboja:** a monarchy in the 6th century BCE but a **sangha** in the Arthashastra.
[23:1:5] ~~ Sources: PMF Ancient & Medieval Part 2 p.19-23; PMF Ancient & Medieval Part 4 (Yaudheyas); old app notes row 23
## SUB 2 | Sources Listing the Mahajanapadas
[23:2:0] TABLE head: Text | Tradition | List
  [23:2:0.0] Anguttara Nikaya (Sutta Pitaka) | Buddhist | 16 Mahajanapadas
  [23:2:0.1] Bhagavati Sutra | Jain | Also 16, some different names; adds Vanga, Malava, Ladha
  [23:2:0.2] Digha Nikaya | Buddhist | Only 12 (omits Ashmaka, Avanti, Gandhara, Kamboja)
  [23:2:0.3] Ashtadhyayi (Panini) | Grammar | 22 janapadas; names Magadha, Ashmaka, Kamboja
[23:2:1] - 16 listed in **Anguttara Nikaya + Bhagavati Sutra** (RAS 2016) – not the Arthashastra or Digha Nikaya.
[23:2:2] - **Panini:** born at **Pushkalavati** (Gandhara); Ashtadhyayi = ~4,000 sutras in **8 chapters**.
[23:2:3] - Anguttara order: Anga, Magadha, Kashi, Kosala, Vajji, Malla, Chedi, Vatsa, Kuru, Panchala, Matsya, Shurasena, Ashmaka, Avanti, Gandhara, Kamboja.
[23:2:4] ~~ Sources: Rocket Indian History Part 2 p.110; PMF Ancient & Medieval Part 2 p.20-21; old app notes row 23
## SUB 3 | Kingdoms and Rulers of the Buddha's Time
[23:3:0] TABLE head: Ruler | Kingdom
  [23:3:0.0] Bimbisara, Ajatashatru | Magadha
  [23:3:0.1] Prasenajit (Pasenadi) | Kosala
  [23:3:0.2] Udayana | Vatsa
  [23:3:0.3] Chanda Pradyota | Avanti (a monarchy)
  [23:3:0.4] Pukkusati | Gandhara
[23:3:1] - **Four great powers:** **Magadha, Kosala, Vatsa, Avanti**; Gandhara was not one. After Magadha, **Kosala** (which absorbed Kashi and the Shakyas) was the strongest – "only Koshala and Magadha emerged as powerful".
[23:3:2] - **Kosala** annexed **Kashi**; Magadha later got it as dowry and by war. **Pradyota** rivalled **Bimbisara and Udayana**.
[23:3:3] - **Udayana:** married **Vasavadatta**, daughter of Pradyota (**Bhasa's Svapnavasavadatta**); the Buddha visited **Kaushambi** in his reign; turned Buddhist under **Pindola Bharadvaja**; donated the **Ghoshitarama** vihara.
[23:3:4] - **Legend links:** **Dasharatha** ruled **Kosala** (Ayodhya); in the Puranas **King Anga** was the father of **Vena** (father of Prithu).
[23:3:5] ~~ Sources: PMF Ancient & Medieval Part 2 p.21-27; Vishnu Purana (Anga–Vena); old app notes row 23
## SUB 4 | Rivers and the South – Assaka on the Godavari
[23:4:0] - **Assaka (Ashmaka):** the **only Mahajanapada south of the Vindhyas**, on the **Godavari** (between the Godavari and Manjira); on the **Dakshinapatha**.
[23:4:1] - Capital **Potana / Potali = Bodhan, Nizamabad (Telangana)**; some place it at **Paithan (ancient Pratishthana)**.
[23:4:2] - **Suttanipata:** sage **Bavari's** ashram on the Godavari in Assaka – earliest Andhra-region link in Buddhist texts.
[23:4:3] TABLE head: Region | River (UPSC 2025)
  [23:4:3.0] Asmaka | Godavari ✔
  [23:4:3.1] Kamboja | Kubha (Kabul), not Vipas ✘
  [23:4:3.2] Avanti | Shipra / Narmada, not Mahanadi ✘
  [23:4:3.3] Kosala | Sarayu ✔ (Ayodhya stands on it)
[23:4:4] > UPSC 2025: only **two** pairs correct.
[23:4:5] ~~ Sources: PMF Ancient & Medieval Part 2 p.23-28; Rocket Indian History Part 2 p.110; old app notes row 23

=== NOTES 1:24 | Second Urbanisation – Nbpw, Punch-marked Coins, Guilds and Towns
## SUB 0 | Guilds (Shreni), Merchants and Trade Terms
[24:0:0] - **Shrenis:** associations of artisans, merchants, moneylenders under elected or hereditary heads (**jetthaka / pamukha**). Craft shrenis trained workers, procured raw materials, sold products; merchant shrenis organised trade.
[24:0:1] - **Powers (UPSC 2012 → II and III):** fixed **wages, rules of work, standards and prices**; had **their own laws (shreni-dharma)** and **judicial powers** over members. They were **autonomous – the king did not interfere**, so "every guild registered with the central authority, king the chief administrator" is **wrong**.
[24:0:2] - **Guilds as banks:** deposits invested at interest – **Nashik inscription** records guild deposits at fixed interest.
[24:0:3] - **Satapatha Brahmana:** first reference to **money-lending**; usurer = **kusidin**.
[24:0:4] TABLE head: Term | Meaning
  [24:0:4.0] Sarthavaha (satthavaha) | Caravan / convoy of merchants, or its leader
  [24:0:4.1] Setthi / Shreshthi | Rich merchant-banker
  [24:0:4.2] Gahapati | Wealthy householder / landowner
  [24:0:4.3] Vanija | Trader
  [24:0:4.4] Kusidin | Usurer
[24:0:5] ~~ Sources: PMF Ancient & Medieval Part 2 p.15-17; NextGen Indian History Part 3 (guilds' own laws); old app notes row 24
## SUB 1 | Punch-marked Coins and Early Money
[24:1:0] - **Punch-marked (Aahat) coins:** **earliest extant coins** of India (7th–6th c. BC to the Mauryan period); **mostly silver**, some copper; symbols only (**sun, animals, trees, hills**), no writing.
[24:1:1] - Called **panas / karshapanas**; issued under Haryankas, Nandas and Mauryas; first definite literary references in **Pali texts**.
[24:1:2] TABLE head: Term | Meaning
  [24:1:2.0] Nishka | Gold / a gold ornament in Vedic texts (nishka-griva = gold necklace); a prestige object, later a unit – not cows or copper/silver coins
  [24:1:2.1] Karshapana / kahapana | Standard silver or copper punch-marked coin
  [24:1:2.2] Satamana | Value or weight unit of 100 mana
  [24:1:2.3] Suvarna | Gold coin (Satavahana usage)
[24:1:3] - **Satavahanas** first used inherited punch-marked coins, then die-struck coins of **lead**, potin, copper, silver; **ship-mast** coins of **Yajnasri Satakarni**.
[24:1:4] - **Gold coins in large numbers:** **Kushanas** (not Mauryas).
[24:1:5] - **Vaishya Tekri** (near Ujjain): Mauryan-era (BCE) stupa bigger than Sanchi; punch-marked and cast Avanti coins; MP to re-excavate it (2026).
[24:1:6] ~~ Sources: PMF Ancient & Medieval Part 1 p.33; PMF Ancient & Medieval Part 2 p.17; Rocket Indian History Part 1 p.55; Rocket Indian History Part 2 (Sheet #90); old app notes row 24
## SUB 2 | Towns and Trade Routes
[24:2:0] TABLE head: Town | Region | Feature
  [24:2:0.0] Rajagriha | Magadha | Ringed by five hills
  [24:2:0.1] Shravasti | Kosala | Crescent-shaped plan
  [24:2:0.2] Kaushambi | Vatsa | On the Yamuna; fortified
  [24:2:0.3] Ujjayini | Avanti | On the Sipra; Ptolemy's "Ozene"
  [24:2:0.4] Taxila | Gandhara | Learning centre; Uttarapatha junction
  [24:2:0.5] Vaishali | Vajji | Republican capital
  [24:2:0.6] Champa | Anga | Near Bhagalpur, on the Ganga
[24:2:1] - **Uttarapatha:** linked **Taxila** (north-west) with the Ganga valley up to Tamralipti.
[24:2:2] - **Dakshinapatha:** southern route to the Deccan (Kaushambi–Ujjayini–Pratishthana), through **Assaka**.
[24:2:3] - AP angle: Satavahana trade centres **Kotilingala, Dhulikatta, Kondapur**.
[24:2:4] ~~ Sources: PMF Ancient & Medieval Part 2 p.17; old app notes row 24
[24:2:5] - **Pali terms for settlements:** **nigama** = (market) town, **nagara** = large city; the Vedic **pur** = fortified settlement.
## SUB 3 | Northern Black Polished Ware and the Second Urbanisation
[24:3:0] - **NBPW:** fine, glossy black, mirror-like **deluxe tableware of the rich**; **c. 700–200 BCE**; **after Painted Grey Ware**; the hallmark of the second urbanisation.
[24:3:1] - **NBPW sites:** mainly the Ganga valley – Hastinapur, Kaushambi, Shravasti, Ayodhya, Varanasi, Rajgir, Pataliputra, Vaishali, Champa; also Ujjain, Vidisha, Taxila (not Lothal).
[24:3:2] TABLE head: Cause | Effect
  [24:3:2.0] Iron tools | Forest clearance in the Ganga plain; surplus
  [24:3:2.1] Trade, crafts, coinage | Taxes and wages in money
  [24:3:2.2] State formation | Mahajanapada capitals became administrative centres
  [24:3:2.3] New merchant classes | Vaishyas backed Buddhism and Jainism
[24:3:3] - **Over 60 towns** in 600–300 BCE; the Mahajanapadas and Magadha = the **second urbanisation**.
[24:3:4] - **Rajghat (Varanasi):** pre-NBPW levels, punch-marked coins, **Greek-inscribed seals**; BHU re-study (Sep 2026) puts urban Varanasi at **c. 800 BCE**.
[24:3:5] TABLE head: Development | Period
  [24:3:5.0] Iron at Mayiladumparai (AMS dating) | c. 2172 BCE; southern Iron Age c. 2100 BCE – older than the Gangetic Iron Age
  [24:3:5.1] Iron ploughshares, axes in the Ganga valley | Later Vedic, c. 1000–600 BCE
  [24:3:5.2] Urban Varanasi | c. 800 BCE
  [24:3:5.3] NBPW | c. 700–200 BCE
  [24:3:5.4] Punch-marked coins begin | 7th–6th c. BCE
  [24:3:5.5] Gold coins in large numbers | Kushanas, 1st–2nd c. CE
[24:3:6] ~~ Sources: PMF Ancient & Medieval Part 2 p.16; NextGen Indian History - APPSC Exams Part 1 (NBP Ware); old app notes row 24

  x: Guilds fixed prices and wages and followed their own shreni-dharma; their head held office by heredity or was chosen by the members, not appointed by the king.

=== NOTES 1:25 | Rise of Magadha – Haryankas, Shishunagas, Nandas and Causes of Supremacy
## SUB 0 | Haryankas – Bimbisara, Ajatashatru, Udayin
[25:0:0] # Bimbisara (c. 545–493 BCE)
[25:0:1] - **Founder of the Haryanka dynasty** (NextGen Rocket; APPSC; SSC CGL 2019) – PMF adds that many scholars give the founding to his grandfather, but exams key **Bimbisara**. Brihadratha belongs to the earlier legendary line.
[25:0:2] - **Real founder of the Magadhan empire**; also called **Seniya / Shrenika** (WBCS 2017).
[25:0:3] - **Contemporary of the Buddha** and of Mahavira (APPSC GS 2018; FRO 2020; Sachivalayam).
[25:0:4]   - **⚠ Disputed:** Ajatashatru was also a contemporary – the Buddha died in his reign – but keys give **Bimbisara** when both are offered.
[25:0:5] - **Conquest:** defeated Brahmadatta of **Anga** and put **Kunika (Ajatashatru)** as viceroy at **Champa**.
[25:0:6] - **Diplomacy:** friendship with **Pradyota of Avanti**; when Pradyota had jaundice, Bimbisara sent his royal physician **Jivaka** to Ujjain.
[25:0:7] - **Jivaka:** physician of Bimbisara's court (UP UDA 2006; State PSC); son of the Rajgriha courtesan **Salavati** (MPPSC 2009) – not Amrapali (Vaishali).
[25:0:8] TABLE head: Queen | Clan / kingdom | Note
  [25:0:8.0] Kosaladevi (Mahakosala) | Kosala, sister of Prasenajit | Brought Kashi as dowry
  [25:0:8.1] Chellana | Lichchhavi of Vaishali | Mother of Ajatashatru (not Udayin)
  [25:0:8.2] Khema | Madra (Punjab) | –
[25:0:9] - **Death:** imprisoned and starved to death by his son **Ajatashatru**.
[25:0:10] # Ajatashatru (c. 493–462 BCE)
[25:0:11] - **Parricide:** killed his father Bimbisara (UPPCS 2011) – Jain name **Kunika**, "Pitruhanta"; he was in turn **killed by his son Udayin** (UPPCS 2007; UPPSC 2007).
[25:0:12] - **Patricide chain (Buddhist texts):** **Bimbisara → Ajatashatru → Udayin**.
[25:0:13] - **Kosala:** war with **Prasenajit** over Kashi; Prasenajit gave his daughter **Vajira** with Kashi as dowry; later annexed Kosala.
[25:0:14] - **Vajji:** annexed after a **16-year** war; minister **Vassakara** split the Lichchhavi chiefs.
[25:0:15] - **War engines:** **mahashilakantaka** (stone-throwing catapult) and **rathamusala** (chariot fitted with a mace).
[25:0:16] - **Buddhism:** met the Buddha (Bharhut relief); hosted the **First Buddhist Council** at Rajgriha.
[25:0:17] # Udayin (c. 462–444 BCE)
[25:0:18] - **Shifted the capital to Pataliputra**; a devout Jain in Jain tradition. Not to be confused with **Udayana of Vatsa**.
[25:0:19] - Probably killed at the urging of **Palaka of Avanti**; his weak successors ended with **Nagadasaka**.
[25:0:20] # Contemporaries
[25:0:21] - **Milinda (Menander)**, the 2nd-century-BCE Indo-Greek, was **not** a contemporary of Bimbisara, the Buddha and **Prasenajit (Kosala)** (IAS 2005).
[25:0:22] > Exam angle: "founder of Haryanka" → **Bimbisara**; "killed his father and was killed by his son" → **Ajatashatru**; "Jivaka" → **Bimbisara's court**.
[25:0:23] ~~ Sources: PMF Ancient & Medieval Part 2 p.99-101; NextGen Rocket Indian History Part 1 p.29-30; NextGen Indian History APPSC Part 2 p.40; britannica.com/biography/Bimbisara; app notes 1:25 (sub 3, 8)
## SUB 1 | Capitals of Magadha – Rajgriha, Pataliputra, Vaishali
[25:1:0] # Capitals in order
[25:1:1] TABLE head: Capital | Ruler | Fact
  [25:1:1.0] Girivraja / Rajgriha (modern Rajgir) | Brihadratha line (Jarasandha); Bimbisara, Ajatashatru | First capital; ringed by five hills, walled – impregnable
  [25:1:1.1] Pataliputra (fort) | Ajatashatru | Fortified the village Pataligrama and built the city as a base against the Vajjis
  [25:1:1.2] Pataliputra (capital) | Udayin | Shifted the capital from Rajgriha to Pataliputra
  [25:1:1.3] Vaishali | Shishunaga | Capital moved there temporarily
  [25:1:1.4] Pataliputra again | Kalashoka | Capital moved back; it stayed the capital of the Mauryas and Guptas
[25:1:2] - **First / earliest / initial capital:** **Girivraja (Rajgriha, Rajgir)** – asked 6 times (State PSC ×3, BPSC 2004, NDA 2001). Not Pataliputra, Vaishali, Champa (capital of **Anga**) or Varanasi.
[25:1:3] - **⚠ Option missing:** a State PSC paper asked the first capital with only Pataliputra, Vaishali, Champa and Anga – none is right; the answer is **Rajgriha**.
[25:1:4] - **Vaishali:** capital of the **Lichchhavi (Vajji) republic**, named after King **Vishal**; near **Mahavira's birthplace**; venue of the **Second** (not First) Buddhist Council.
[25:1:5] - **Not a Magadhan capital:** **Kaushambi** – capital of **Vatsa** (CGPSC 2011).
[25:1:6] # Who founded Pataliputra
[25:1:7] - **Ajatashatru** fortified Pataligrama and **built the city of Pataliputra** – the answer when Udayin is not offered (APPSC G1 2002 and G1 PYQ: Ajatashatru; Dhanyakataka, Nalanda, Lumbini are distractors).
[25:1:8] - **Udayin** made it the **capital**; Jain texts call him the **founder of Pataliputra** – the answer when Ajatashatru is not offered ("who founded Pataliputra": Udayin, not Ashoka, Bimbisara, Mahapadma).
[25:1:9] - **⚠ Disputed:** with both offered, most books and keys give **Ajatashatru** for the city and **Udayin** for the capital.
[25:1:10] - **"First chose Pataliputra as capital":** **Udayin** (not Ajatashatru, Kalashoka or Kanishka).
[25:1:11] # Pataliputra – site and remains
[25:1:12] - **Site:** modern **Patna**, at the **confluence of the Ganga and the Son**; surrounded by rivers – a **water-fort (jaladurga)**.
[25:1:13] - **Excavated remains:** **Kumrahar** (Mauryan pillared hall) and **Bulandibagh** (wooden palisade) – both = Pataliputra, not Vaishali, Kapilavastu, Shravasti or Varanasi.
[25:1:14] - **Decline:** Hiuen-Tsang (7th c. CE) found it in ruins.
[25:1:15] # Rajgir today
[25:1:16] - **Vishwa Shanti Stupa:** the World Peace Pagoda on **Ratnagiri hill, Rajgir** – billed as the highest in the world (BPSC 2008).
[25:1:17] - **Rajgriha** was also the place of **Mahavira's first sermon** and of the **First Buddhist Council** (Saptaparni cave).
[25:1:18] > Exam angle: "first capital" → **Rajgriha**; "built Pataliputra / Pataligrama fort" → **Ajatashatru**; "made it the capital" → **Udayin**; Kumrahar, Bulandibagh → **Pataliputra**.
[25:1:19] ~~ Sources: PMF Ancient & Medieval Part 2 p.100-101, p.103; NextGen Indian History APPSC Part 2 p.40-41; app notes 1:25 (sub 0, 6); nalanda.nic.in (Vishwa Shanti Stupa)
[25:1:20] - **Not Magadhan:** **Tughlaqabad** (Delhi) was built by **Ghiyasuddin Tughlaq**, not Firoz Shah Tughlaq; Udayin succeeded Ajatashatru.
## SUB 2 | Shishunagas and Nandas
[25:2:0] # Shishunaga dynasty (c. 413–345 BCE)
[25:2:1] - **Shishunaga:** a viceroy at Banaras / amatya **chosen by the people** after Udayin's weak successors; moved the capital to **Vaishali**.
[25:2:2] - **Malwa:** Shishunaga **destroyed Avanti** (capital **Ujjayini**) and annexed Avanti and Vatsa – the expansion over Malwa (UP Lower 2008 ×2).
[25:2:3] - **Kalashoka:** moved the capital **back to Pataliputra**; **Second Buddhist Council** at Vaishali (383 BCE).
[25:2:4] - **End:** the last Shishunaga, **Mahanandin**, was killed by **Mahapadma Nanda**.
[25:2:5] # Conquests in order
[25:2:6] TABLE head: Ruler | Annexed
  [25:2:6.0] Bimbisara | Anga
  [25:2:6.1] Ajatashatru | Kosala, Vajji
  [25:2:6.2] Shishunaga | Avanti, Vatsa
  [25:2:6.3] Mahapadma Nanda | Kalinga, Kshatriya kingdoms of the north
[25:2:7] # Nanda dynasty (c. 345–321 BCE)
[25:2:8] - **Sequence:** Haryanka → **Shishunaga → Nanda → Maurya** (State PSC; BPSC 2005).
[25:2:9] - **Mahapadma Nanda:** founder; **first non-Kshatriya dynasty**; **first empire-builder** of India; titles **Ekarat** (sole sovereign) and **Sarvakshatrantaka** (destroyer of Kshatriyas) → hence **Aparoparashurama**, "a second Parashurama" (UPUDA 2010).
[25:2:10] - **Hathigumpha inscription** of Kharavela: a **Nanda king dug a canal in Kalinga** and carried off a Jina image (UPPCS 1999).
[25:2:11] - **Dhana Nanda:** **last Nanda** (RRB 2001); Greek **Agrammes / Xandrames**; ruled at **Alexander's invasion** (326 BCE) – the dynasty ruling North India then was the **Nanda** (UPSC 2000).
[25:2:12] - **Fall:** Chandragupta Maurya, with Chanakya, overthrew Dhana Nanda in **c. 321 BCE**.
[25:2:13] - **Army (Greek accounts):** about 200,000 infantry, 60,000 cavalry, 3,000–6,000 elephants.
[25:2:14] > Exam angle: "after the Shishunagas" → **Nandas**; "after the Nandas" → **Mauryas**; "Malwa added" → **Shishunaga**; "Aparoparashurama" → **Mahapadma Nanda**.
[25:2:15] ~~ Sources: PMF Ancient & Medieval Part 2 p.101-102, p.105-106; NextGen Indian History APPSC Part 2 p.40-41; app notes 1:25 (sub 4, 5)
## SUB 3 | Causes of Magadhan Supremacy
[25:3:0] - **Most powerful and richest Mahajanapada** (6th–4th c. BCE); it became the base of the **Mauryan Empire**. Puranas: the first dynasty was the **Brihadratha** (Jarasandha ruled from **Girivraj**).
[25:3:1] TABLE head: Factor | Effect
  [25:3:1.0] Iron mines nearby (Jharkhand) | Weapons and tools; forest clearing
  [25:3:1.1] Alluvial soil, ample rainfall | Agricultural surplus, taxes
  [25:3:1.2] Ganga and Son (navigable) | Cheap transport and trade
  [25:3:1.3] Elephants from the eastern forests | First large-scale use of war elephants
  [25:3:1.4] Naturally fortified capitals | Rajgriha (five hills), Pataliputra (water-fort)
  [25:3:1.5] Unorthodox society | Little Vedic hold; ambitious rulers and ministers
[25:3:2] - **⚠ Not a cause:** control of western sea ports, gold mines, superior cavalry or a republican system.
[25:3:3] - **Buddhist councils linked to Magadhan kings:** First – **Ajatashatru** (Rajgriha); Second – **Kalashoka** (Vaishali); Third – **Ashoka** (Pataliputra).
[25:3:4] ~~ Sources: PMF Ancient & Medieval Part 2 p.103-104; NextGen Indian History APPSC Part 1 p.67; app notes 1:25 (sub 0, 2, 7)

  Alexander built a canal connecting the Ganga and Son | *Nandaraja dug a canal which was later extended by Kharavela | Shishunaga constructed irrigation works in Avanti | Bimbisara constructed a canal in Anga
  x: The Hathigumpha inscription states that a canal dug by Nandaraja, identified with Mahapadma Nanda, was extended by Kharavela. It also indicates Nanda control over Kalinga.
[f4213ddda80ff] sub2
  Which was the first non-Kshatriya dynasty of Magadha?
  *Nanda | Shishunaga | Haryanka | Maurya
  x: The Nandas were the first non-Kshatriya dynasty.
[g9d626d9f1169] sub2
  Consider the following statements:
1. Shishunaga destroyed the power of Avanti.
2. Kalashoka of the Shishunaga dynasty held the Second Buddhist Council at Vaishali.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
  1 only | 2 only | *Both 1 and 2 | Neither 1 nor 2
  x: Shishunaga ended Avanti's power and his successor Kalashoka held the Second Buddhist Council at Vaishali.
[gd1a40896c0df] sub2
  Which Nanda king took the image of Jina from Kalinga?
  Dhana Nanda | *Mahapadma Nanda | Ajatashatru | Kalashoka
  x: Mahapadma Nanda took the statue of Jina from Kalinga, which shows Nanda control over Kalinga.
[ne0dd98e61ed] sub3
  Which of the following was NOT a factor in the rise of Magadha?
  Availability of iron ore for tools and weapons | *Dependence on a purely pastoral economy | Rivers providing transport and defence | Access to elephants for warfare
  x: Magadha benefited from fertile agriculture, iron resources, elephants and rivers. Dependence on a purely pastoral economy was not a stated factor in its rise.
[n90a6876bf8f] sub3
  By which period had Magadha become the most powerful Mahajanapada?
  8th–7th centuries BCE | 3rd–2nd centuries BCE | 1st century BCE–1st century CE | *6th–4th centuries BCE
  x: Magadha became the most powerful Mahajanapada during the 6th to 4th centuries BCE.
[naff999b5aa1] sub3
  Which empire emerged from the political base created by the rise of Magadha?
  Kushana Empire | *Mauryan Empire | Satavahana kingdom | Indo-Greek kingdom
  x: Magadha's supremacy, built on resources, geography, military power and able rulers, became the base of the Mauryan Empire.
[nindian-history-139-18] sub3
  From which place did Jarasandha of the Brihadratha dynasty rule?
  Pataliputra | *Girivraj | Champa | Vaishali
  x: Jarasandha of the Brihadratha dynasty ruled from Girivraj, as the Sabha Parva of the Mahabharata relates.
[faea0b2f38983] sub3
  According to the Puranas, which was the first dynasty of Magadha?
  Haryanka | *Brihadratha | Shishunaga | Nanda
  x: The Puranas name the Brihadratha as Magadha's first dynasty; the Haryankas came later under Bimbisara.
[g892972498edb] sub3
  Which was the most powerful and richest Mahajanapada of the 6th–4th century BCE?
  Kosala | Vatsa | *Magadha | Avanti
  x: Magadha was the most powerful and richest Mahajanapada and became the base of the Mauryan Empire.
[n9dfce17f41d] sub3
  Which animals from the east strengthened the military power of Magadha?
  Horses | *Elephants | Camels | Bullocks
  x: Eastern elephants, nearby iron ore and Ganga-Son river transport contributed to Magadhan supremacy.
[n68e8e8bc809] sub3
  In contrast to the Vedic heartland, what was the nature of the society of Magadha?
  Dominated by Vedic orthodoxy | Matriarchal | *Unorthodox, with little Vedic hold | Purely pastoral
  x: Magadha's society was relatively unorthodox with little Vedic hold; iron, rivers, elephants and defensible capitals supported its rise.
[n03c8b288bf9] sub3
  Which geographical combination best explains Magadha's economic and strategic advantage?
  *Alluvial soil, ample rainfall and navigable rivers | Coastal ports, desert routes and salt mines | Arid soil, scarce rainfall and mountain passes | Black soil, western seaports and snow-fed rivers
  x: Alluvial soil and ample rainfall supported agriculture, while the Ganga and Son aided transport and trade.
[g29284339ca3e] sub3
  Which of the following was NOT a cause of the rise of Magadha?
  *Control of the western sea ports | Iron ore deposits nearby | Elephants from the east | The navigable Ganga and Son rivers
  x: Magadha rose because of nearby iron ore, elephants, fertile soil and navigable rivers; western sea ports were not a factor.
[g67737647b7eb] sub3
  Match the following:
List-I (Buddhist Council)
A. First Council
B. Second Council
C. Third Council
List-II (Ruler)
1. Ashoka
2. Ajatashatru
3. Kalashoka
Select the correct answer using the code given below:
  A-3, B-2, C-1 | A-2, B-1, C-3 | *A-2, B-3, C-1 | A-1, B-3, C-2
  x: The First Council was held under Ajatashatru, the Second under Kalashoka and the Third under Ashoka.

=== NOTES 1:26 | Jainism – Tirthankaras, Mahavira, Triratna, Panchamahavratas, Anekantavada and Syadvada
## SUB 0 | Tirthankaras – First, 23rd, Last and Their Symbols
[26:0:0] # The 24 Tirthankaras
[26:0:1] - **Tirthankara** = "ford-maker", one who shows the path across the cycle of rebirth – a **Jain** term (UPPCS 1993). **Jina** = conqueror (of passions).
[26:0:2] - **Historicity:** only the last two, **Parshvanatha (23rd)** and **Mahavira (24th)**, are confirmed by Jain and Buddhist texts; the first 22 are uncertain.
[26:0:3] - **First:** **Rishabhadeva (Adinatha / Rishabhanatha)**, born at **Ayodhya** to Nabhi and Marudevi (APPSC G1; CGPSC 2013).
[26:0:4] - **Last (24th):** **Vardhamana Mahavira** (APPSC FBO; IAS 1993; WBPCS 2008) – a **reformer** of an older faith, not its founder (CDS 2011).
[26:0:5] - **Originator of Jainism:** Jain tradition → **Rishabhadeva** (UPPSC 2010).
[26:0:6]   - **⚠ Disputed:** historians treat Parshvanatha as the first historical teacher and some books call Mahavira the founder.
[26:0:7] - **20th:** **Munisuvrata (Munisuvrat Swami)** (APPSC Extension Officer).
[26:0:8] TABLE head: No. | Tirthankara | Symbol | Nirvana / place
  [26:0:8.0] 1 | Rishabhadeva | Bull | Mount Kailash (Ashtapada); born Ayodhya
  [26:0:8.1] 2 | Ajitanatha | Elephant | Sammed Shikhar
  [26:0:8.2] 12 | Vasupujya | Buffalo | Champapuri (not Pavapuri)
  [26:0:8.3] 13 | Vimalanatha | Boar (not buffalo) | Sammed Shikhar
  [26:0:8.4] 20 | Munisuvrata | Tortoise | Sammed Shikhar
  [26:0:8.5] 21 | Naminatha | Blue lotus | Sammed Shikhar
  [26:0:8.6] 22 | Neminatha (Arishtanemi) | Conch | Girnar (Urjayanta)
  [26:0:8.7] 23 | Parshvanatha | Serpent | Sammed Shikhar
  [26:0:8.8] 24 | Mahavira | Lion | Pavapuri
[26:0:9] - **Incorrect pair (SI 2023):** Vimalanatha – Buffalo ✗ (boar).
[26:0:10] - **Not Tirthankaras:** **Nathamuni** (Sri Vaishnava acharya; UPPCS 2004) and **Bahubali (Gomateshwara)**, son of Rishabhadeva – so "Gomateshwara represents the last Tirthankara" is the wrong statement (UPSC). Chandraprabha (8th), Sambhava (3rd) and Nemi (22nd) are Tirthankaras.
[26:0:11] # Parshvanatha (23rd)
[26:0:12] - **Born at Varanasi** to **King Asvasena and Queen Vama** – associated mainly with **Varanasi** (UPPSC 2016; IAS 2003).
[26:0:13] - Renounced at **30**; enlightenment after **84 days** of penance; died at **100**, about **250 years before Mahavira**.
[26:0:14] - **Nirvana at Sammed Shikhar** (Parasnath hill, **Jharkhand**) → a Jain **Siddha Kshetra** for him (UPPCS 2002 ×2); Pava = Mahavira, Champa = Vasupujya, Urjayanta = Neminatha.
[26:0:15] - Taught **four vows (chaturyama)**; believed in the eternity of matter; his followers wore **white**.
[26:0:16] > Exam angle: first → **Rishabha**; 23rd → **Parshvanatha (Varanasi, Sammed Shikhar)**; last → **Mahavira**; Bahubali is **not** a Tirthankara.
[26:0:17] ~~ Sources: NextGen Indian History APPSC Part 1 p.42-43; PMF Ancient & Medieval Part 2 p.71, p.81, p.83; app notes 1:26 (sub 0)
## SUB 1 | Life of Mahavira
[26:1:0] TABLE head: Aspect | Detail
  [26:1:0.0] Birth | Kundagrama (Kundalpur / Basukunda) near Vaishali; 599 BCE (traditional) or 540 BCE (NextGen, PMF)
  [26:1:0.1] Father | Siddhartha, chief of the Jnatrika Kshatriya clan
  [26:1:0.2] Mother | Trishala, Lichchhavi princess, sister of Chetaka
  [26:1:0.3] Varna | Kshatriya (APPSC G1)
  [26:1:0.4] Wife, daughter | Yashoda; daughter Anojja (Priyadarshana) – Svetambara view; Digambaras say he never married
  [26:1:0.5] Renunciation | At 30; one garment for 13 months, then a naked monk; 12 years of austerity
  [26:1:0.6] Kaivalya | At 42, under a sal tree at Jrimbhikagrama on the Rijupalika (UP RO 2017)
  [26:1:0.7] Death | Pava (Pavapuri) near Rajgriha, aged 72; 527 BCE (traditional) or 468 BCE (NextGen)
[26:1:1] - **Died at:** **Pavapuri** – not Rajgir (the nearby town), Sanchi, Ranchi or Samastipur (BPSC 2002; State PSC ×2).
[26:1:2] - **Birthplace:** **Kundagrama / Kundalpur** (UPPCS 2004; State PSC) – not Vaishali itself, Pataliputra or Magadha.
[26:1:3] - **"Not true" (CGPSC 2022):** "He did not marry" ✗ – he married Yashoda; the 24th Tirthankara ✓, Trishala Chetaka's sister ✓, died 527 BCE at Pavapuri ✓.
[26:1:4] - **Titles:** Mahavira (great hero), **Jina** (conqueror), **Nirgrantha** (free from bonds), Kevalin, Arhat – **Tathagata** is the Buddha's.
[26:1:5] - **Preaching:** 30 years in Kosala, Magadha and further east; first sermon at **Rajgriha**; visited the courts of Bimbisara and Ajatashatru.
[26:1:6] # Family and Lichchhavi links
[26:1:7] - **Chetaka:** chief of the **Lichchhavis** of Vaishali (RAS 2013: Chetak–Lichchhavi ✓); Mahavira's **maternal uncle**; his daughter **Chellana** married Bimbisara.
[26:1:8] - **IAS 2003:** "Mahavira's mother was the **daughter** of Chetaka" ✗ (she was his **sister**); Buddha's mother was a **Koliya** princess ✓; Parshvanatha of **Banaras** ✓ → **II and III**.
[26:1:9]   - **⚠ Disputed:** the Digambara tradition makes Trishala Chetaka's daughter; the UPSC key follows "sister".
[26:1:10] - **Lichchhavi republic:** first to recognise Jainism.
[26:1:11] # Disciples
[26:1:12] - **Jamali**, his nephew and son-in-law: **first disciple / follower** (UPPCS 2008; SSC 2013) – not Yashoda (wife), Anojja (daughter) or Trishala (mother).
[26:1:13] - **11 Ganadharas**; only **Arya Sudharma** survived him and became the **first Thera (head of the Sangha)** (MPPSC 2009); **Bhadrabahu** was the 6th Thera.
[26:1:14] # Contemporaries
[26:1:15] TABLE head: Person | Who
  [26:1:15.0] Gautama Buddha | Contemporary teacher (NDA 2009) – Parshvanatha (earlier), Bhadrabahu and Chandragupta (later) are not
  [26:1:15.1] Bimbisara, Ajatashatru | Magadha
  [26:1:15.2] Chetaka | Lichchhavis
  [26:1:15.3] Pradyota | Avanti
  [26:1:15.4] Prasenajit | Kosala
  [26:1:15.5] Makkhali Gosala | Companion for about six years; founder of the Ajivikas (niyati)
  [26:1:15.6] Ajita Kesakambalin | Materialist teacher
[26:1:16] ~~ Sources: NextGen Indian History APPSC Part 1 p.42-44, p.47; PMF Ancient & Medieval Part 2 p.69-70; NextGen Rocket Indian History Part 1 p.80, p.122; app notes 1:26 (sub 1, 2); gktoday.in (UPSC 2003 key)
## SUB 2 | Anekantavada, Syadvada and Their Exponents
[26:2:0] # Doctrines
[26:2:1] TABLE head: Doctrine | Meaning
  [26:2:1.0] Anekantavada | Reality is many-sided; no single view holds the whole truth – basis of tolerance and pluralism
  [26:2:1.1] Syadvada | Relativity of knowledge: every statement is prefixed with syat ("maybe", "in some respect") – conditioned predication
  [26:2:1.2] Saptabhanginaya | The seven-fold predication of Syadvada
  [26:2:1.3] Nayavada | Theory of partial standpoints
[26:2:2] - **Two main doctrines of Jain philosophy:** Anekantavada and Syadvada ✓; every thing has many properties, the permanent one being its **attribute (guna)** ✓ (CGPSC 2023 → **both**). Everything that exists has **substance (dravya), quality (guna) and mode (paryaya)**.
[26:2:3] - **Core theory of Jainism:** **Anekantavada** (UPSC 2009; JPSC 2011); **Syadvada** is Jain (UKPSC 2005; APPSC G1 1987).
[26:2:4] - **Jain vs Buddhist doctrines (RAS 2021):** Anekantavada, Syadvada = **Jain**; **Sarvastivada, Sunyavada = Buddhist**; niyati = Ajivika; materialism = Charvaka.
[26:2:5] # Exponents
[26:2:6] TABLE head: Person | Fact
  [26:2:6.0] Kundakundacharya | Digambara monk; propounded / preached Syadvada (APPSC 2008 ×2) and Anekantavada; wrote Samayasara (APPSC G2 2008 ×2), Pravachanasara, Panchastikayasara, Niyamasara; ashram at Konakondla (Anantapur); first Jain monk of Andhra
  [26:2:6.1] Somadeva Suri | Title "Syadvadachala Simha" (APPSC G2 2011 ×3); wrote Yasastilaka Champu, Nitivakyamrita
  [26:2:6.2] Kalakasuri | Jain monk honoured at the Satavahana court (Pratishthana)
[26:2:7] - **⚠ Trap:** Kundakunda **preached** Syadvada; Somadeva Suri only **held the title**. Nagarjuna and Bavari are Buddhists.
[26:2:8] - **Yogasara** is not Kundakunda's – APPSC keys it to **Nagarjuna** (Buddhist).
[26:2:9] > Exam angle: "preached Syadvada" / "Samayasara" → **Kundakundacharya**; "Syadvadachala Simha" → **Somadeva Suri**.
[26:2:10] ~~ Sources: PMF Ancient & Medieval Part 2 p.72; NextGen Indian History APPSC Part 1 p.52; NextGen AP History previous papers p.8, p.18, p.46; NextGen Satavahanas p.28; NextGen Rocket AP History p.46; app notes 1:26 (sub 3)
## SUB 3 | Triratna, Vows and Jain Practices
[26:3:0] # Triratna (Ratnatraya) – the path to moksha
[26:3:1] - **Right faith (belief), right knowledge, right conduct (action)** – the Jain three jewels (UPPCS 2004; State PSC; CGPSC 2017 ×2).
[26:3:2] - **⚠ Not a Jain jewel:** **right view** – the first step of the Buddhist Eightfold Path (SSC 2011).
[26:3:3] - **⚠ Disputed:** Buddhism also has a Triratna (**Buddha, Dhamma, Sangha** – the three refuges); with Jainism offered, keys give **Jainism**. Nyaya and Yoga have none.
[26:3:4] - **Match (APPSC G2):** Jainism – **Triratna**; Buddhism – **Ashtanga Marga**; Saivism – **Linga**; Animism – **Nature worship** → a-iii, b-ii, c-iv, d-i.
[26:3:5] # Five vows
[26:3:6] TABLE head: Vow | Meaning | Given by
  [26:3:6.0] Ahimsa | Non-violence | Parshvanatha
  [26:3:6.1] Satya | Truth | Parshvanatha
  [26:3:6.2] Asteya | Non-stealing | Parshvanatha
  [26:3:6.3] Aparigraha | Non-possession | Parshvanatha
  [26:3:6.4] Brahmacharya | Celibacy (chastity) | Added by Mahavira
[26:3:7] - **Mahavratas** = great vows of monks; **Anuvratas** = small vows of householders – a **Jain** concept (UPSC 1995 ×2; CGPSC 2013).
[26:3:8] - **Householders** also gave cooked food to the needy daily; strict followers avoided **agriculture**.
[26:3:9] # Practices
[26:3:10] TABLE head: Practice | Meaning
  [26:3:10.0] Sallekhana / Santhara / Samadhi Marana | Ritual fast unto death at life's end – Jain (CGPSC 2015 ×2); Chandragupta Maurya at Shravanabelagola
  [26:3:10.1] Paryushana | Festival; last day Samvatsari = forgiveness day
  [26:3:10.2] Pratikramana | Self-review
  [26:3:10.3] Padmasana, Kayotsarga | Postures of Tirthankara images
[26:3:11] - **Digambara** = sky-clad; the **Agamas** are in **Ardhamagadhi**.
[26:3:12] ~~ Sources: NextGen Indian History APPSC Part 1 p.44-46; PMF Ancient & Medieval Part 2 p.71, p.83; PMF Indian Art & Culture Part 5 (anuvratas); app notes 1:26 (sub 5, 6, 7)
## SUB 4 | Soul, Karma and the Uncreated Universe
[26:4:0] - **Souls everywhere:** all objects, even **rocks, stones and running water**, have a soul and feel injury → extreme ahimsa (UPSC 2023 ×2: **Jainism**).
[26:4:1] - **No creator:** the world is **not created** by a supreme being; it runs by an **eternal law** of decay and development → "**Universal Law**" (UPSC 2011). The universe is **eternal**, with **no final annihilation** (UPPCS 2014: Jainism); Jainism is non-theistic.
[26:4:2] - **Four forms of existence:** **Deva** (gods), **Manushya** (humans), **Naraki** (hell beings), **Tiryancha** (animals and plants) – **not Yaksha** (UPSC 2026).
[26:4:3] TABLE head: Term | Meaning
  [26:4:3.0] Jiva / Ajiva | Soul / non-conscious matter
  [26:4:3.1] Karma | Material bondage of the soul, cause of rebirth
  [26:4:3.2] Samvara | Stopping the inflow of karma
  [26:4:3.3] Nirjara | Shedding existing karma by penance
  [26:4:3.4] Kaivalya (Kevala jnana) | Complete knowledge, omniscience (IAS 1993)
  [26:4:3.5] Siddha | Fully liberated soul
  [26:4:3.6] Tattva | Origination, decay, permanence
[26:4:4] - **Salvation:** only through **monastic life** – householders cannot attain it; the Vedas and Brahmana supremacy are rejected.
[26:4:5] ~~ Sources: NextGen Indian History APPSC Part 1 p.44, p.46; PMF Ancient & Medieval Part 2 p.69-72; app notes 1:26 (sub 4)
## SUB 5 | Jainism and Buddhism Compared
[26:5:0] TABLE head: Point | Jainism | Buddhism
  [26:5:0.0] Penance | Extreme path, self-mortification | Middle Way – avoids extremes
  [26:5:0.1] Soul | Eternal jiva in all things | Anatta (no-self)
  [26:5:0.2] Ahimsa | Stricter, even in thought | Important, less strict
  [26:5:0.3] Goal | Kevala jnana, moksha | Nirvana
  [26:5:0.4] Spread | Mostly within India | Widely abroad
[26:5:1] - **Common to both:** **karma and transmigration** (UPPCS 1996), **indifference to the Vedas**, **denial of rituals**, **non-injury to animals**, both Kshatriya-led shramana movements.
[26:5:2] - **Not common:** **self-mortification** (BPSC 2001; State PSC) and **avoiding the extremes** of penance and enjoyment (Buddhist only) → UPSC 2012: **II and III**; UPSC 1996: **II, III and IV**.
[26:5:3] - **⚠ Trap:** "both men and women could attain salvation" and "salvation only after death" are not shared beliefs.
[26:5:4] - **Decline of Jainism:** severe austerities, loss of royal patronage, the Digambara–Svetambara split.
[26:5:5] ~~ Sources: PMF Ancient & Medieval Part 2 p.70, p.84; NextGen Indian History APPSC Part 1 p.44, p.52; app notes 1:26 (sub 8)
## SUB 6 | Early Jainism in Andhra
[26:6:0] TABLE head: Place / fact | Detail
  [26:6:0.0] Vaddamanu (Guntur) | Basadi of Samprati, Ashoka's grandson ("Ashoka of Jainism"); Kharavela's vihara nearby
  [26:6:0.1] Danavulapadu (YSR Kadapa) | Jain kshetra of Rashtrakuta Indra III
  [26:6:0.2] Rayalaseema (Kurnool, Anantapur) | "Jain corridor" on the Bhadrabahu–Chandragupta route to Shravanabelagola
  [26:6:0.3] Hanamkonda inscription | Prolaraju's record opens with a Jain prayer – the early Kakatiyas were Jains
[26:6:1] - **Mahapadma Nanda** is said to have conquered Andhra and spread Jainism there.
[26:6:2] - **Ikshvaku** royal women (Chantisiri, Bodhisiri) backed **Buddhism** at Nagarjunakonda, not Jainism.
[26:6:3] ~~ Sources: NextGen Satavahanas p.27-28; app notes 1:26 (sub 9)

  x: Mahavira's symbol is the lion; the bull is Rishabhadeva's, the serpent Parshvanatha's and the conch Neminatha's.

  x: Rishabhadeva - bull, Munisuvrata - tortoise, Neminatha - conch, Mahavira - lion.

  x: Rishabhadeva was born at Ayodhya to Nabhi and Marudevi; Parshvanatha was born at Varanasi and Mahavira at Kundagrama.

  x: Anekantavada is the manifold nature of reality; Syadvada is conditioned predication and Nayavada the theory of partial standpoints.

  x: Nayavada is the theory of partial standpoints; niyati (fate) is Ajivika and anatmavada (no-self) Buddhist.

  x: Kundakundacharya preached Syadvada; Sunyavada and Vijnanavada are Buddhist, and Niyati is Ajivika.

  x: Syadvada ('syat' = may be) is the relativity of knowledge, with seven-fold predication; many-sidedness is Anekantavada and partial standpoints is Nayavada.

  x: Syadvada, the relativity of knowledge, is expressed as Saptabhanginaya, a seven-fold predication.

  x: Santhara (Sallekhana) is a Jain ritual fast unto death at the end of life to purify karma (APPSC G2 2017). It is not a Buddhist, Sufi or Parsi practice.

[ga753348cbad3] sub4

  x: In Jainism, tattva is utpada-vyaya-dhrauvya: origination, decay and permanence.

=== NOTES 1:27 | Jain Councils, Digambara–svetambara Split, Jain Literature, Spread and Royal Patrons
## SUB 0 | Jain Monuments and Pilgrim Sites
[27:0:0] TABLE head: Site | State | Fact
  [27:0:0.0] Shravanabelagola – Gommateshwara (Bahubali) | Karnataka | 57-ft monolith raised c. 981 CE by Chamundaraya, minister of Western Ganga Rachamalla IV (UP Lower 2009 ×2; IAS 1994)
  [27:0:0.1] Dilwara temples, Mount Abu | Rajasthan | Built of marble (UPPCS 2010); Vimala Shah (Solanki minister), 1031
  [27:0:0.2] Udayagiri–Khandagiri | Odisha | Rock-cut caves of Kharavela
  [27:0:0.3] Prabhasgiri (Pabhosa) | Uttar Pradesh, near Kaushambi | Jain site, linked with the 6th Tirthankara Padmaprabha (UPPCS 2008)
  [27:0:0.4] Kaushambi | Uttar Pradesh | Sacred to both Buddhists and Jains – the Buddha's Ghoshitarama; birthplace of Padmaprabha (UPUDA 2010; RAS 2007)
  [27:0:0.5] Sammed Shikhar | Jharkhand (Parasnath hill) | Nirvana of Parshvanatha and 19 other Tirthankaras
[27:0:1] - **Mahamastakabhisheka:** the anointing of **Bahubali (Gommateshwara)** every **12 years** at Shravanabelagola (UPSC 2009 ×2) – not the Buddha, Mahavira or Nataraja. The 88th was held in **February 2018**.
[27:0:2] - **Odd one out (APPSC JL 2003):** Amaravati, Nagarjunakonda, Ajanta = Buddhist; **Gomateshwara** = Jain.
[27:0:3] - **⚠ Trap:** Bahubali, son of Rishabhadeva, is **not** a Tirthankara; Sarnath and Kushinagar are Buddhist only.
[27:0:4] ~~ Sources: PMF Ancient & Medieval Part 2 p.83; NextGen Indian History APPSC Part 3 (Gommateswara table); jainheritagecentres.com (Kaushambi); app notes 1:27 (sub 6)
[27:0:5] - **Palitana** (Shatrunjaya hill, near **Bhavnagar**, Gujarat): the largest cluster of **Jain temples** (Svetambara), not Mount Abu (Dilwara).
## SUB 1 | Jain Councils and the Canon
[27:1:0] # The two councils
[27:1:1] TABLE head: Council | Place, date | President | Outcome
  [27:1:1.0] First | Pataliputra, c. 300 BCE (Chandragupta Maurya's reign) | Sthulabhadra | Teachings arranged into 12 Angas; boycotted by Bhadrabahu's returning monks → first split
  [27:1:1.1] Second | Vallabhi (Valabhi), Gujarat, 512 CE (Maitraka patronage) | Devardhi Kshamashramana | Canon finally compiled and written down in Ardhamagadhi; Upangas added; 12th Anga lost
[27:1:2] - **First council:** **Pataliputra** (UKPSC 2021; UPPCS 2004) – not Vaishali, Mathura, Ujjain, Vallabhi, Abu or Pava.
[27:1:3] - **Svetambara Agama finally edited:** **Valabhi (Balabhi)** (UPPCS 2008).
[27:1:4] - **CGPSC 2020:** "Svetambara canon includes 12 Angas" ✓; "Angas compiled at Vallabhi" ✗ (compiled at Pataliputra; Vallabhi only recompiled them) → **I true, II false**.
[27:1:5] - **Andhra:** a **Jain conference at Venkatathatipuri** (Satavahana era), presided over by **Arhabali** – not one of the two councils.
[27:1:6] # The canon
[27:1:7] TABLE head: Term | Fact
  [27:1:7.0] Agamas / Siddhanta | Sacred books of the Jains (APPSC G2 2011), compiled by the Ganadharas
  [27:1:7.1] 14 Purvas | Earliest scriptures, preached by all Tirthankaras; held in the 12th Anga (Drishtivada); now lost
  [27:1:7.2] 12 Angas | Main body of the canon – Acharanga, Sutrakritanga, Bhagavati …
  [27:1:7.3] Ang-bahya | 12 Upangas, 10 Prakirnas, 6 Chhedasutras, 4 Mulasutras
  [27:1:7.4] Language | Ardhamagadhi Prakrit (UPPSC 2006) – not Pali (Buddhist) or Sanskrit
[27:1:8] - **Earliest holy book:** the **Fourteen Purvas** (State PSC) – not the 12 Angas or Upangas.
[27:1:9] - **Acharanga Sutra:** code of conduct for monks; **Bhagavati Sutra:** doctrines.
[27:1:10] - **Digambaras** hold that the Purvas and original Angas were **all lost** and reject the Svetambara canon; their chief texts are the Satkhandagama and Kasaya-pahuda.
[27:1:11] - **Not Jain:** **Therigatha** – verses of Buddhist nuns in the Pali Khuddaka Nikaya (UPSC 1996); Acharanga, Sutrakritanga and Brihatkalpa are early Jain texts.
[27:1:12] ~~ Sources: NextGen Indian History APPSC Part 1 p.46, p.48; PMF Ancient & Medieval Part 2 p.53, p.77-78, p.83; app notes 1:27 (sub 0, 2)
## SUB 2 | Spread of Jainism and Royal Patrons
[27:2:0] # Spread
[27:2:1] - **South:** during a **12-year famine** in Magadha (Chandragupta Maurya's reign) **Bhadrabahu** led monks with Chandragupta to **Shravanabelagola** – he is credited with taking Jainism south (CDS 2008), not Sthulabhadra, Sudharma or Indrabhuti.
[27:2:2] - **Kalinga:** under **Kharavela** (c. 1st century BCE); his **Hathigumpha** inscription records Jain patronage.
[27:2:3] - **Early Christian era centres:** **Mathura and Ujjain**.
[27:2:4]   - **APPSC G1 2004:** the centre that grew under **Vajramuni** (1st–2nd c. CE) is keyed **Kausali** (official key; not confirmed in the notes).
[27:2:5] - **Later:** Karnataka (Gangas, Kadambas, Rashtrakutas), Tamil country, Gujarat, Rajasthan, Malwa; it survives in **Gujarat, Rajasthan, Karnataka and Tamil Nadu**, mainly among **traders**.
[27:2:6] - **UPSC 2004:** Kharavela's patronage (1st c. BCE) ✓; "spread south under Sthulabhadra" ✗; "Bhadrabahu's followers = Svetambaras" ✗; "early Jains worshipped images, unlike Buddhists" ✗ (initially neither worshipped images).
[27:2:7] # Royal patrons
[27:2:8] TABLE head: Patron | Dynasty | Jain link
  [27:2:8.0] Udayin | Haryanka | Devout Jain (Jain tradition)
  [27:2:8.1] Chandragupta Maurya | Maurya | Disciple of Bhadrabahu; sallekhana at Shravanabelagola
  [27:2:8.2] Samprati | Maurya, Ashoka's grandson | "Ashoka of Jainism"
  [27:2:8.3] Kharavela | Chedi (Mahameghavahana), Kalinga | Ardent Jain (APPSC G2 Mains 2025; CDS 2006)
  [27:2:8.4] Simuka (Srimukha) | Satavahana | First Satavahana Jain patron; 52 army chiefs built 52 temples (Kalpa Pradeepa)
  [27:2:8.5] Amoghavarsha I | Rashtrakuta | Patron of Jinasena
  [27:2:8.6] Indra III | Rashtrakuta | Danavulapadu
  [27:2:8.7] Chamundaraya | Minister of Western Ganga Rachamalla IV | Gommateshwara statue
  [27:2:8.8] Kumarapala | Chalukya (Solanki) of Gujarat | Patron of Hemachandra
[27:2:9] - **Kharavela's dynasty:** **Chedi** (UPPCS 2015) – Kalinga was his kingdom, not his dynasty.
[27:2:10] - **Not Jain patrons:** Pulakeshin II (Brahmanical); Bimbisara is claimed by both Jains and Buddhists.
[27:2:11] - **Samrat Samprati Museum:** inaugurated by the PM at **Koba Tirth, Gandhinagar (Gujarat)** on **31 March 2026** (Mahavir Jayanti).
[27:2:12] - **Migration route:** Magadha → **Rayalaseema (Kurnool, Anantapur)** → Shravanabelagola.
[27:2:13] ~~ Sources: NextGen Indian History APPSC Part 1 p.47-48; PMF Ancient & Medieval Part 2 p.73; NextGen Rocket Indian History Part 1 p.31; pmindia.gov.in (Samrat Samprati Museum, 31 Mar 2026); app notes 1:27 (sub 4, 5)
## SUB 3 | Jain Literature, Authors and Language
[27:3:0] TABLE head: Work | Author / note
  [27:3:0.0] Jinendra Kalyanabhyudayamu | Appayacharya, in Kakatiya Prataparudra's time (APPSC ×2) – not Pampa
  [27:3:0.1] Parishishtaparvan (Sthaviravali Charitra) | Hemachandra, 12th c. – famine story and early Jain teachers
  [27:3:0.2] Trishashtilakshana Mahapurana | Jain (Jinasena–Gunabhadra; Kannada Chavundaraya Purana by Chamundaraya)
  [27:3:0.3] Kalpasutra | Bhadrabahu
  [27:3:0.4] Samayasara, Pravachanasara | Kundakundacharya (Saraswati gachcha at Konakondla)
  [27:3:0.5] Tattvartha Sutra | Umasvati – accepted by both sects
  [27:3:0.6] Adipurana (Mahapurana) | Jinasena
  [27:3:0.7] Kalyanakaraka (medicine) | Ugradityacharya
  [27:3:0.8] Yasastilaka Champu | Somadeva Suri
[27:3:1] - **UPSC 2022 ×2:** Jain texts = **Parishishtaparvan and Trishashtilakshana Mahapurana** (2 and 4 only); **Nettipakarana and Avadanashataka are Buddhist**.
[27:3:2] # Language
[27:3:3] - **Early Jains discarded Sanskrit** and preached in **Prakrit**; the Agamas are in **Ardhamagadhi**.
[27:3:4] - **Apabhramsha:** the Jains composed its **earliest important works** and its **first grammar** – so "they never used Apabhramsa for the earliest important works" is the **incorrect** statement (APPSC G1).
[27:3:5] - Jain writers enriched **Prakrit, Apabhramsa, Kannada and Telugu**.
[27:3:6] # Social base and decline
[27:3:7] - **Traders and merchants** formed the social base (war and agriculture were barred); stressed **action / conduct** over birth.
[27:3:8] - **Decline:** lack of **missionary zeal**, loss of royal patronage, sectarian split, severe austerity, Bhakti revival.
[27:3:9] ~~ Sources: PMF Ancient & Medieval Part 2 p.78-79, p.83-84; app notes 1:27 (sub 3, 8)
## SUB 4 | Jainism in Andhra and Telangana
[27:4:0] # Royal patronage and centres
[27:4:1] - **Early Kakatiyas** patronised **Jainism** (APPSC; Junior Asst) before turning to Shaivism; Andhra Jainism was mainly **Digambara** → "Jainism of the Digambara sect". Source: **Siddeshwara Charitra**.
[27:4:2] - **Potla Cheruvu (Patancheru)** was a popular **Jain** centre in the **Eastern Chalukya** period (APPSC G2 2017 ×2).
[27:4:3] - **Jain kshetras of Telangana:** Bodhan, Vemulawada, Potlacheruvu, Anmakonda (Hanamkonda), Kolanupaka.
[27:4:4] TABLE head: Centre | Note
  [27:4:4.0] Nedumba Vasati, Bejawada | Built by Ayyana Mahadevi, wife of Eastern Chalukya Vishnuvardhana
  [27:4:4.1] Kathikabharana Jinalaya, Dharmavaram | Durgaraja
  [27:4:4.2] Ramatirtham (Vizianagaram) | Hill centre; Ugradityacharya wrote Kalyanakaraka
  [27:4:4.3] Kadalalaya Basadi | Mailama, wife of Kakatiya minister Betana (1117 CE)
  [27:4:4.4] Aryavatam, Kolanupaka, Munugodu | Other Jain centres
[27:4:5] - Under the Eastern Chalukyas, **Shudras** took up Jainism for social advancement; a Jain monk fled **Rajaraja Narendra's** persecution to Hanamkonda (**Orugallu Kaifiyat**).
[27:4:6] - **Vijayanagara:** **Devaraya II** built a Jinalaya in the Pan Supari Bazar.
[27:4:7] - Jainism held rocky **Rayalaseema**; **Buddhism** held the Krishna–Godavari delta.
[27:4:8] # Decline
[27:4:9] - **Serious blow:** the **Virashaiva (Lingayat)** movement (APPSC G1) – Kalamukhas and Vira Shaivas persecuted Jains (Brahmaya at Govur and Potlacheru; **Ganapatideva** at Anmakonda).
[27:4:10] - **Karnataka:** **Basavanna's** 12th-century Lingayat movement; **Tamil country:** the **Alvars and Nayanars**.
[27:4:11] ~~ Sources: NextGen AP History previous papers p.8, p.46; NextGen AP History Part 11 p.11-12; NextGen Satavahanas p.27; app notes 1:27 (sub 7)
## SUB 5 | Digambara–Svetambara Split and Sub-sects
[27:5:0] # The split
[27:5:1] - **Story:** after the 12-year famine, **Bhadrabahu's** monks returned from the south, still naked and strict; those who stayed under **Sthulabhadra** wore **white** → **Digambaras** (sky-clad) and **Svetambaras** (white-clad), c. 300 BCE.
[27:5:2] - **First founder of the Svetambara creed:** **Sthulabhadra** (RAS 1999) – Bhadrabahu led the Digambaras.
[27:5:3] TABLE head: Aspect | Digambara | Svetambara
  [27:5:3.0] Leader | Bhadrabahu | Sthulabhadra
  [27:5:3.1] Dress | Sky-clad monks | White robes
  [27:5:3.2] Women | Must be reborn as men for moksha | Can attain liberation
  [27:5:3.3] Mahavira | Never married | Married Yashoda
  [27:5:3.4] Monk's items | Broom and water pot | 14 items incl. clothes
  [27:5:3.5] Canon | Original Angas lost | Keep the 12 Angas
[27:5:4] # Sub-sects
[27:5:5] TABLE head: Sub-sect | Branch | Feature
  [27:5:5.0] Sthanakvasi | Svetambara | Non-idolatrous, prayer halls (sthanakas); Lonka Shah – a Jain sect (UPSC 2018)
  [27:5:5.1] Terapanthi | Svetambara (from the Sthanakvasis) | Acharya Bhikshu, 18th c.
  [27:5:5.2] Murtipujaka | Svetambara | Idol-worshipping original Svetambaras
  [27:5:5.3] Yapaniya | Grew from the Digambaras | Bridge sect; some Svetambara views; nude images; active in Karnataka and Andhra – a Jain sect (UPPCS 2010)
  [27:5:5.4] Bisapantha, Terapantha, Taranapantha | Digambara | Taranapanthis worship books, not idols
[27:5:6] ~~ Sources: NextGen Indian History APPSC Part 1 p.48-49; PMF Ancient & Medieval Part 2 p.73-76; app notes 1:27 (sub 1)

  x: Jinendra Kalyanabhyudayamu was written by Appayacharya and dedicated to Kakatiya Prataparudra. Adipurana is by Jinasena, Yasastilaka Champu by Somadeva Suri, and Harivamsha Purana is the Jain version of the Mahabharata.
[ff746dd5795b2] sub3
  Who wrote the Tattvartha Sutra, accepted by both Jain sects?
  *Umasvati | Jinasena | Somadeva Suri | Bhadrabahu
  x: Umasvati wrote the Tattvartha Sutra, which both Digambaras and Svetambaras accept.
[f2aaa73babbb0] sub3
  Who wrote the Adipurana (Mahapurana) on the life of Rishabha?
  Hemachandra | *Jinasena | Chamundaraya | Appayacharya
  x: Jinasena, patronised by Amoghavarsha I, wrote the Adipurana.
[f1acc7b61864a] sub3
  Who wrote the Jinendra Kalyanabhyudayamu, dedicated to Kakatiya Prataparudra?
  Ugradityacharya | Jinasena | Umasvati | *Appayacharya
  x: Appayacharya wrote the Jinendra Kalyanabhyudayamu and dedicated it to Prataparudra.
[fd18af54ec9c3] sub3
  Where did Kundakundacharya found the Saraswati gachcha?
  Ramatirtham | *Konakondla | Danavulapadu | Vaddamanu
  x: Kundakundacharya founded the Saraswati gachcha at Konakondla in Anantapur district.
[f8a7c2a503b0a] sub3
  Who is the author of the Kalpasutra?
  Kundakundacharya | Umasvati | Hemachandra | *Bhadrabahu
  x: The Kalpasutra (Jinacharitra, Sthaviravali, Samachari) is by Bhadrabahu.
[geddcf589c766] sub3
  Who wrote the Kalyanakaraka, a Jain work on medicine?
  Appayacharya | Umasvati | Jinasena | *Ugradityacharya
  x: Ugradityacharya wrote the Kalyanakaraka at Ramatirtham; Appayacharya wrote the Jinendra Kalyanabhyudayamu.
[g93b8e0bcf512] sub3
  Which work was written by Kundakundacharya, who founded the Saraswati gachcha at Konakondla?
  Tattvartha Sutra | *Samayasara | Parishishtaparvan | Kalpasutra
  x: Kundakundacharya wrote the Samayasara; the Tattvartha Sutra is by Umasvati, the Parishishtaparvan by Hemachandra and the Kalpasutra by Bhadrabahu.
[gdc69dc0032b3] sub3
  Who wrote the Trishashtilakshana Mahapurana (Chavundaraya Purana) in Kannada?
  Hemachandra | *Chamundaraya | Jinasena | Ugradityacharya
  x: The Trishashtilakshana Mahapurana, also called Chavundaraya Purana, was written by Chamundaraya.
[n1635143ad6f] sub3
  In which language did Jain writers produce the earliest important works and the first grammar?
  Pali | *Apabhramsa | Tamil | Sanskrit
  x: Jains wrote the earliest important Apabhramsa works and its first grammar; their canon is in Ardhamagadhi Prakrit.
[n821fccac235] sub3
  Jain literature contributed to the enrichment of which group of languages?
  Pali, Greek, Kannada and Marathi | *Prakrit, Apabhramsa, Kannada and Telugu | Telugu, Urdu, Persian and Prakrit | Sanskrit, Tamil, Pali and Persian
  x: Jain literature enriched Prakrit, Apabhramsa, Kannada and Telugu.
[n2943504b7a1] sub3
  The lack of which of the following was a factor in the decline of Jainism?
  Austerity | *Missionary zeal | Sectarian divisions | Rival Bhakti movements
  x: Lack of missionary zeal, along with loss of royal patronage, sectarian split, severe austerity and Bhakti revival, led to Jainism's decline.
[n52a4861be21] sub3
  Which social group formed the principal social base of Jainism in urban and commercial centres?
  *Traders and merchants | Military aristocrats | Pastoral chiefs | Forest tribes
  x: Traders, merchants and commercial communities supported Jainism, particularly in urban centres.
[nd24aa72b502] sub3
  Challenging caste hierarchy, what did Jainism stress over birth?
  Ritual | Lineage | *Action | Wealth
  x: Jainism challenged birth-based hierarchy, stressed conduct and action, and promoted vegetarianism.
[fcf9dc793f86c] sub3
  In which language did the early Jains preach to reach the common people?
  Sanskrit | Pali | Tamil | *Prakrit
  x: Early Jains used popular Prakrit, not Sanskrit, to reach common people.
[n930cf5b2435] sub4
  In the Eastern Chalukya period in Andhra, which group adopted Jainism for social advancement?
  Brahmanas | *Shudras | Foreign merchants | Rajputs
  x: In Andhra under the Eastern Chalukyas, Shudras adopted Jainism as a means of social advancement.
[na58122b1478] sub4
  Who built the Nedumba Vasati at Bejawada?

  x: Nedumba Vasati at Bejawada, or Vijayawada, was built by Ayyana Mahadevi, the wife of Eastern Chalukya Vishnuvardhana.
[n566c2ae57b4] sub4

  x: Durgaraja built the Kathikabharana Jinalaya at Dharmavaram, not Danavulapadu.
[n94b53da4f3c] sub4
  Which source records the flight of a Jain monk to Hanamkonda from Rajaraja Narendra's persecution?
  *Orugallu Kaifiyat | Siddeshwara Charitra | Kalpa Pradeepa | Prataparudriyam
  x: The Orugallu Kaifiyat records the monk's flight; Siddeshwara Charitra mentions Ganapatideva's persecution of Jains.
[n728f7f22aab] sub4
  Who built the Kadalalaya Basadi, known from the 1117 CE Padmakshi inscription?
  Ayyana Mahadevi, wife of Vishnuvardhana | *Mailama, wife of the Kakatiya minister Betana | Durgaraja, under Vimaladitya | Devaraya II of Vijayanagara
  x: Mailama, wife of the Kakatiya minister Betana, built the Kadalalaya Basadi (1117 CE Padmakshi inscription). Ayyana Mahadevi built the Nedumbasadi at Bejawada, Durgaraja the Kathikabharana Jinalaya at Dharmavaram, and Devaraya II a Jinalaya in Pan Supari Bazar.
[fa68d1c4c4fb6] sub4
  Ramatirtham, an Eastern Chalukya Jain hill centre, is in which district?
  Kadapa | Anantapur | Kurnool | *Vizianagaram
  x: Ramatirtham (Ramagiri) is in Vizianagaram; Danavulapadu is in Kadapa and Konakondla in Anantapur.
[f3c148e3f6404] sub4

  x: The Siddeshwara Charitra says the early Kakatiyas were Jains and that Ganapatideva later persecuted them.
[fd18b049cb01b] sub4

  Prataparudra | Vimaladitya | *Ganapatideva | Devaraya II
  x: The Siddeshwara Charitra records Ganapatideva's persecution of Jains; Vimaladitya was Eastern Chalukya and Devaraya II of Vijayanagara.
[n5b314dc8396] sub4

  x: Potla Cheruvu was a prominent Jain centre during the Eastern Chalukya period.

  Buddhism | Vaishnavism | Ajivika | *Jainism
  x: The early Kakatiyas, as feudatories of the Western Chalukyas, were Jains (Siddeshwara Charitra) and later became Shaivas.
[gd39f23c27e20] sub4

  x: Devaraya II of Vijayanagara built a Jinalaya in the Pan Supari Bazar.
[gdfb57c09e1b2] sub4
  While Jainism held the rocky Rayalaseema, which religion held the Krishna–Godavari delta in early Andhra?
  Lokayata | Kapalika | Ajivika | *Buddhism
  x: Jainism held rocky Rayalaseema and the north coastal borderlands; Buddhism held the Krishna–Godavari delta.
[g88bd5a3fb32c] sub4
  Which of the following was a Jain centre in Andhra?
  Bhattiprolu | *Munugodu | Salihundam | Thotlakonda
  x: Munugodu, like Aryavatam and Kolanupaka, was a Jain centre; the others are Buddhist sites.
[gefdbc2b1eaef] sub4

  x: Basavanna's Lingayat movement of the 12th century weakened Jainism in Karnataka.
[gce2ef341f370] sub4

  x: In Tamil Nadu the Bhakti saints, the Alvars and Nayanars, weakened Jainism.
[n34ab2e9ee74] sub5
  Which Jain leader is associated with the group that remained in Magadha?
  Bhadrabahu | Devardhi Kshamashramana | *Sthulabhadra | Kundakundacharya
  x: Sthulabhadra led the group that stayed in Magadha, while Bhadrabahu led the group that went south.
[n45cf012526f] sub5
  Which of the following pairs of Jain sect and feature is correctly matched?
  Digambara – White robes | *Terapanthi – Acharya Bhikshu | Sthanakvasi – Sky-clad monks | Svetambara – Opposition to idol worship
  x: Terapanthi was founded by Acharya Bhikshu. Digambaras are sky-clad, Svetambaras wear white robes and Sthanakvasis oppose idol worship.
[n403eec99da1] sub5
  Which one of the following statements about the Digambara and Svetambara sects is NOT correct?
  *Digambaras hold that women can attain liberation in their existing body. | Svetambaras accept that women can attain liberation. | Digambara monks are described as sky-clad. | Svetambara monks wear white robes.
  x: Digambara doctrine holds that women must be reborn as men before attaining moksha; Svetambaras accept the possibility of liberation for women.
[ne9f4e32bafa] sub5
  Consider the following statements about the Jain Angas:
1. Digambaras preserve all twelve original Angas.
2. The Svetambara canon includes twelve Angas.
Which of the above statements is/are correct?
  1 only | *2 only | Both 1 and 2 | Neither 1 nor 2
  x: The Svetambara canon includes twelve Angas, while Digambaras hold that all the original Angas were lost.
[f79280643c567] sub5
  Which of the following pairs of Jain sub-sect and feature is correctly matched?
  Terapanthi – bridge sect between the two main sects | Yapaniya – founded by Acharya Bhikshu | *Sthanakvasi – against idol worship | Sthanakvasi – Digambara offshoot
  x: The Sthanakvasis, a Svetambara offshoot, oppose idol worship; Yapaniya is the bridge sect and Acharya Bhikshu founded the Terapanthis.
[f27717ab1d5e4] sub5
  Which Jain sect, a bridge between Digambaras and Svetambaras, was active in Andhra?
  *Yapaniya | Sthanakvasi | Terapanthi | Svetambara
  x: The Yapaniyas tried to reconcile both sects and were active in Andhra through the Yapanacharyas.
[fb90b022ce91e] sub5
  Which of the following is NOT correct about the Digambara sect?
  Its monks are sky-clad | *Mahavira married Yashoda | Women must be reborn as men to attain moksha | Its monks keep only a broom and a water pot
  x: Digambaras hold that Mahavira never married; it is the Svetambaras who say he married Yashoda.
[ffc85790e1d2b] sub5
  A Svetambara monk may keep how many items, including clothes?
  12 | 10 | *14 | 6
  x: Svetambara monks keep 14 items including clothes, while Digambara monks keep only a broom and a water pot.
[f890076e2b699] sub5
  Who founded the Terapanthi sub-sect of Jainism in the 18th century?
  Lonka Shah | Sthulabhadra | Kundakundacharya | *Acharya Bhikshu
  x: Acharya Bhikshu founded the Terapanthi offshoot of the Svetambaras; Lonka Shah is linked with the Sthanakvasis.
[g518b7b48feea] sub5
  Who founded the Sthanakvasi sect of Jainism, which opposes idol worship?
  Devardhi Kshamashramana | *Lonka Shah | Sthulabhadra | Acharya Bhikshu
  x: The Sthanakvasis, a Svetambara offshoot against idol worship, are linked with Lonka Shah; Acharya Bhikshu founded the Terapanthis.

=== NOTES 1:28 | Buddhism – Life of the Buddha, Four Noble Truths, Eightfold Path, Pratityasamutpada, Sangha
## SUB 0 | Places of the Buddha's Life – Lumbini, Bodh Gaya, Sarnath, Kushinagar
[28:0:0] TABLE head: Event | Place | Today / marker
  [28:0:0.0] Birth | Lumbini grove (near Kapilavastu) | Rumindei / Rummindei, Nepal; Ashoka's inscribed pillar records "here the Buddha Shakyamuni was born"
  [28:0:0.1] Great Renunciation | Kapilavastu | –
  [28:0:0.2] Enlightenment | Uruvela = Bodh Gaya, under the Bodhi (pipal) tree, on the Niranjana (Lilajan) | Bihar; Mahabodhi Temple
  [28:0:0.3] First sermon | Deer Park (Mrigadava), Sarnath = Rishipatana, near Varanasi | UP; Ashoka's Lion Capital (State Emblem)
  [28:0:0.4] Mahaparinirvana (death) | Kusinara / Kushinagar (Kasia), capital of the Mallas | Deoria–Kushinagar district, UP; Mahaparinirvana Temple
[28:0:1] - **Match (APPSC ASO, CDS 2011):** birth – **Lumbini**, enlightenment – **Bodh Gaya**, first sermon – **Sarnath**, death – **Kushinagar**; Rajgriha is the spare distractor.
[28:0:2] - **Kushinagar:** the **Malla republic**, not the Lichchhavis or Sakyas; the Buddha died there at **80** under sal trees.
[28:0:3] - **Mahaparinirvana Temple, Kushinagar:** reclining dying Buddha of **Chunar sandstone**, unearthed **1876–77**.
[28:0:4] - **Niranjana river:** only **Bodh Gaya** of the sacred places stands on it (not Kushinagar, Lumbini, Rishipatana).
[28:0:5] - **Rummindei pillar** marks the **birth** (Ashoka visited in his 20th regnal year); the king whose inscription names Lumbini = **Ashoka**.
[28:0:6] - **"Birthplace of Buddhism":** APPSC G2 2011 calls **Sarnath** the "birthplace of Buddhism" – the **Dharma** and **Sangha** began there with the first sermon; Lumbini is only the Buddha's birthplace.
[28:0:7] - **Ashtamahasthanas** (eight great sites): **7 in India**, Lumbini (Nepal) the eighth.
[28:0:8] # Bodh Gaya facts
[28:0:9] - **Mahabodhi Temple:** UNESCO World Heritage Site since **2002**.
[28:0:10] - Original Bodhi tree destroyed by **Shashanka** (7th c., per **Hiuen Tsang**; earlier Pushyamitra Sunga by legend); the present tree was planted under **Alexander Cunningham** (1881) and is called the **fifth generation** (Bihar Tourism) – some count it the sixth, so BPSC dropped the question.
[28:0:11] - **Maha Bodhi Society (1891):** **Anagarika Dharmapala**, to restore Bodh Gaya to Buddhists.
[28:0:12] > Lumbini = birth; Bodh Gaya = enlightenment (Niranjana); Sarnath = first sermon; Kushinagar = death (Mallas).
[28:0:13] ~~ Sources: NextGen Indian History - APPSC Exams Part 1 p.63; PMF Ancient & Medieval Part 2 p.42-43; PMF Art & Culture Part 5 p.73-74; old app notes row 28 (sub 2)
## SUB 1 | Core Teachings – Nirvana, Karma, Anatta, Middle Path
[28:1:0] TABLE head: Term | Meaning
  [28:1:0.0] Nirvana (nibbana) | Main goal; "extinction of the flame of desire"; end of suffering and of rebirth
  [28:1:0.1] Middle Path (Madhyama Marga) | Avoids both self-indulgence and self-mortification – the path to nirvana, not the goal
  [28:1:0.2] Anicca | Impermanence – "the world is unstable and transient"
  [28:1:0.3] Anatta / Anatmavada | No permanent self (soul) – a Buddhist doctrine, not Sankhya, Vedanta or Jain
  [28:1:0.4] Dukkha | Suffering
  [28:1:0.5] Karma | Intention-based morality; present state from past actions; rebirth till nirvana
  [28:1:0.6] Raft simile | Teaching as a raft – upaya (skilful means), not dogma
[28:1:1] - **Three marks:** Anicca, Anatta, Dukkha.
[28:1:2] - **Beliefs (UPPSC 1992):** karma ✓, rebirth ✓, nirvana ✓; **God neither affirmed nor denied** – belief in God ✗; belief in God not needed for the Sangha.
[28:1:3] - **Rejected Vedic authority**, animal sacrifice and ritual; stressed **purity of conduct / a moral life** over philosophical problems (the seed of later Buddhist philosophy lies in his own teachings – CGPSC 2023: both true).
[28:1:4] - **Caste (UPPSC 1998):** Buddhism **did not abolish caste and class** as social facts, **challenged the Brahmins' top rank** (Kshatriyas listed first), and texts call some **jatis and crafts (hina-sippa) low** – exam key: all three true (PMF instead says the Buddha condemned varna).
[28:1:5] - **Statements on the Buddha:** born in (the Sakya state of) **Kapilavastu** – strictly at Lumbini near it; enlightened at **Bodh Gaya**; **discarded the Vedic religion**; preached the **noble truths** – key: all four.
[28:1:6] > Nirvana = goal; Middle Path = means; Anatta = no soul; God neither affirmed nor denied.
[28:1:7] ~~ Sources: NextGen Indian History - APPSC Exams Part 1 p.65-66; PMF Ancient & Medieval Part 2 p.45-46; old app notes row 28 (sub 6)
## SUB 2 | Life Story – Dates, Renunciation, Epithets and Texts
[28:2:0] - **Born 563 BC** (traditional; PMF "566–563 BCE"), Vaishakha Purnima; **died 483 BCE** at 80 (NextGen: c. 486 BC).
[28:2:1] - **Four sights:** old man, sick man, corpse, ascetic → **Mahabhinishkramana** (Great Renunciation = **departure from home**) at **29**, with charioteer **Channa** and horse **Kanthaka**.
[28:2:2] - **Six years** of austerities with **five ascetics**; tempted by **Mara**; **enlightenment at 35** on the 49th day of meditation.
[28:2:3] - **Last words:** **"appamadena sampadetha"** – "all composite things decay; **strive diligently**".
[28:2:4] # Epithets
[28:2:5] TABLE head: Epithet | Belongs to
  [28:2:5.0] Buddha (Awakened), Tathagata ("thus-gone"), Shakyamuni (sage of the Shakyas), Mahamuni | Gautama Buddha
  [28:2:5.1] "Light of Asia" | Buddha – Edwin Arnold's poem (1879, subtitle "The Great Renunciation"), based on the Lalitavistara
  [28:2:5.2] Jina, Kevalin, Nigantha Nataputta, Nayaputta | Mahavira
  [28:2:5.3] Prachhanna Buddha ("crypto-Buddhist") | Shankaracharya, not the Buddha
[28:2:6] - **UPSC 2024:** Shakyamuni ✓, Tathagata ✓, Nayaputta ✗ → "2 and 3 only".
[28:2:7] # Texts on his life
[28:2:8] - **Buddhacharita** (Ashvaghosha, Sanskrit) = the **life history of the Buddha**; **Jatakas** = stories of his **previous births** (in the Sutta Pitaka); **Tripitakas** = the canon.
[28:2:9] > 29 – 35 – 80 = Renunciation – Enlightenment – Parinirvana.
[28:2:10] ~~ Sources: NextGen Indian History - APPSC Exams Part 1 p.63, p.66; NextGen Indian History - APPSC Exams Part 2 p.21 (Ashvaghosha); Rocket Indian History Part 1 p.40 (Buddhacharita – life of Buddha); PMF Ancient & Medieval Part 2 p.42-44; old app notes row 28 (sub 1, 5)
## SUB 3 | Family, Teachers and Disciples
[28:3:0] TABLE head: Relation | Person
  [28:3:0.0] Name / clan | Siddhartha Gautama, Sakya Kshatriya clan of Kapilavastu
  [28:3:0.1] Father | Suddhodana, chief of the Sakyas
  [28:3:0.2] Mother | Maya (Mahamaya), a Koliya princess – died days after his birth
  [28:3:0.3] Foster-mother | Mahapajapati (Prajapati) Gotami
  [28:3:0.4] Wife / son | Yashodhara / Rahula
  [28:3:0.5] Charioteer, horse | Channa (Chhandaka), Kanthaka
  [28:3:0.6] Cousin | Devadatta – wanted to lead the Sangha in the Buddha's lifetime
  [28:3:0.7] Teachers | Alara Kalama (meditation), Uddaka Ramaputta
[28:3:1] - **Not in the Buddha's life (CDS 2013):** **Goshala Maskariputra (Makkhali Gosala)** – the **Ajivika** leader; Kanthaka, Channa, Alara Kalama are.
[28:3:2] # Disciples
[28:3:3] TABLE head: Person | Role
  [28:3:3.0] Mahakassapa | Presided over the First Council (Saptaparni Cave, Rajagriha, under Ajatashatru)
  [28:3:3.1] Ananda | Personal attendant; recited the Sutta; got women admitted
  [28:3:3.2] Upali | Recited the Vinaya
  [28:3:3.3] Sariputta, Mahamoggallana | Chief disciples; relics at Sanchi
  [28:3:3.4] Subhadda | Last convert – ordained at Kushinagar on the Buddha's last night
  [28:3:3.5] Tapassu, Bhallika | First lay disciples (merchants)
[28:3:4] - **No successor:** the Buddha **nominated nobody** (not Ananda, Mahakassapa or Upali) – the **Dhamma and Vinaya** were to be the teacher.
[28:3:5] - **Current (May–June 2026):** relics of **Sariputta and Mahamoggallana** taken from **Sanchi to Mongolia** (Gandan monastery, Ulaanbaatar) for Buddha Purnima by the Ministry of Culture, then returned.
[28:3:6] > Maya = mother (Koliya); Mahapajapati = foster-mother; Devadatta = rival cousin; Subhadda = last convert.
[28:3:7] ~~ Sources: NextGen Indian History - APPSC Exams Part 1 p.63; PMF Ancient & Medieval Part 2 p.42; PMF Art & Culture Part 5 p.73; newsonair.gov.in (1 June 2026, relics in Mongolia); old app notes row 28 (sub 0, 10)
## SUB 4 | Sangha, Triratna and Monastic Terms
[28:4:0] - **Triratna** (three jewels / refuges) = **Buddha, Dhamma, Sangha** – not the Tripitaka, not Sheel-Samadhi-Sangha; not Trivarga or Trimurti.
[28:4:1] - **Match (APPSC G2 Mains 2025):** Jainism – **Triratna**; Buddhism – **Ashtanga Margam**; Saivism – **Linga**; Animism – **Nature worship**.
[28:4:2] - **Ajivika – Niyati; Charvaka – materialism; Jainism – Anekantavada; Buddhism – Ashtangika Marga.**
[28:4:3] # Admission
[28:4:4] - Open to all **above 15**, **caste no bar**; barred: **criminals, lepers, those with infectious diseases**; slaves, debtors, children needed permission.
[28:4:5] - **Women** admitted at **Vaishali** at the request of **Ananda** and **Mahapajapati Gotami** – the **first nun** (not Shravasti).
[28:4:6] - **Sanghamitta** took the Bhikkhuni Sangha to **Sri Lanka**; it lasted till the **Chola invasion of 1017 AD**.
[28:4:7] # Terms
[28:4:8] TABLE head: Term | Meaning
  [28:4:8.0] Bhikkhu / Bhikkhuni | Monk / nun living on alms
  [28:4:8.1] Pravrajya | Going forth (head shaved, robes)
  [28:4:8.2] Upasampada | Full ordination
  [28:4:8.3] Uposatha | Fortnightly confession and recitation of rules
  [28:4:8.4] Vassa | 3-month rainy-season retreat
  [28:4:8.5] Pavarana | Confession of offences by monks at the end of the Vassa (UPSC 2002)
  [28:4:8.6] Vihara / Chaitya / Stupa | Monks' residence / prayer hall / relic mound
  [28:4:8.7] Upasaka / Upasika | Lay follower
  [28:4:8.8] Donors | Anathapindika – Jetavana; Vishakha – Pubbarama; Bimbisara – Venuvana
[28:4:9] - **AP angle:** sage **Bavari's** disciples from the **Godavari** region brought Buddhism to **Andhra**.
[28:4:10] > Triratna = Buddha-Dhamma-Sangha; Pavarana = end-of-Vassa confession; nuns admitted at Vaishali.
[28:4:11] ~~ Sources: NextGen Indian History - APPSC Exams Part 1 p.67; PMF Ancient & Medieval Part 2 p.47-49; PMF Art & Culture Part 5 p.77; old app notes row 28 (sub 10)
[28:4:12] - **Parivrajaka** = renunciant wanderer; **Shramana** = wandering ascetic outside the Vedic order – **not** a high-status priest (UPSC 2016 → "I and III only").
## SUB 5 | First Sermon – Dhammachakkappavattana
[28:5:0] - **Name:** **Dhammachakra Pravartana / Dhammacakkappavattana Sutta** = "setting in motion the **Wheel of Dharma**" – the **first sermon at Sarnath** (not his "sight", norms or rituals).
[28:5:1] - **Theme:** the **Four Noble Truths** and the **Eightfold Path** – so the Eightfold Path "forms the theme of" the **Dhammacakkappavattana Sutta** (UPSC 1998), not the Dipavamsa, Divyavadana or Mahaparinibbana Sutta.
[28:5:2]   - **Mahaparinibbana Sutta** = his last journey and death; **Brahmajala Sutta** = first sutta of the Digha Nikaya.
[28:5:3] - **Audience:** the **five former companions** – **Kondanna (Kaundinya), Bhaddiya, Vappa, Mahanama, Assaji (Ashvajit)** – who became **arhats**.
[28:5:4] - **Vesak / Buddha Purnima** (Vaisakha full moon) marks **birth, enlightenment and Mahaparinirvana** – **not the first sermon**.
[28:5:5] > Dhammachakra = first sermon = Sarnath = Four Truths + Eightfold Path.
[28:5:6] ~~ Sources: NextGen Indian History - APPSC Exams Part 1 p.63; PMF Ancient & Medieval Part 2 p.43; PMF Art & Culture Part 5 p.74; old app notes row 28 (sub 3)
## SUB 6 | Other Sites – Shravasti, Vaishali, Rajgir and the Kingdoms
[28:6:0] TABLE head: Place | Association
  [28:6:0.0] Shravasti (Kosala capital) | Most sermons; Jetavana Vihara (Anathapindika); Twin Miracle
  [28:6:0.1] Vaishali (Lichchhavis) | Last rainy season (at Beluva, a suburb); women admitted to the Sangha; monkey's honey offering; Second Council
  [28:6:0.2] Rajagriha (Rajgir) | Taming of the elephant Nalagiri; Venuvana; First Council
  [28:6:0.3] Sankasya | Descent from the Trayastrimsa heaven
  [28:6:0.4] Pava | Fell ill after Sukaramaddava offered by Chunda
[28:6:1] - **Council venues:** **Rajgir, Vaishali, Pataliputra, Kashmir** – not Sarnath, Bodh Gaya or Kushinagar.
[28:6:2] - **Pairs (UPPSC 2004):** Lothal – dockyard ✓, Sarnath – first sermon ✓, Nalanda – seat of Buddhist learning ✓; **Rajgir – Lion Capital ✗** (the Lion Capital is at **Sarnath**).
[28:6:3] # Kingdoms and royal followers
[28:6:4] - **Magadha:** **Bimbisara, Ajatashatru**; **Kosala:** **Prasenjit** (a disciple with his family); **Vatsa / Kausambi:** **Udayana**.
[28:6:5] - **UPSC 2015 – kingdoms associated with the Buddha's life:** **Kosala and Magadha** (official key); PMF also counts **Chanda Pradyota of Avanti** a disciple, but Avanti and **Gandhara** are not in the key.
[28:6:6] > Shravasti = most sermons; Vaishali = last rainy season + nuns; Rajgir = Nalagiri + First Council.
[28:6:7] ~~ Sources: NextGen Indian History - APPSC Exams Part 1 p.63; PMF Ancient & Medieval Part 2 p.43-44 (UPSC 2015 key); old app notes row 28 (sub 1, 2, 5)
## SUB 7 | Four Noble Truths and the Eightfold Path
[28:7:0] TABLE head: Truth | Content
  [28:7:0.0] 1. Dukkha | The world is full of suffering ("sabbam dukkham")
  [28:7:0.1] 2. Samudaya | Suffering has a cause – craving (tanha), ignorance, attachment
  [28:7:0.2] 3. Nirodha | Cessation of suffering = Nirvana
  [28:7:0.3] 4. Magga | The path to cessation = the Eightfold Path
[28:7:1] - **Order:** suffering → cause → cessation → path = **A-D-B-C / I-IV-II-III** (UPPSC 2006).
[28:7:2] - **Buddha and Mirabai** both held the **world full of sorrow** (first Noble Truth).
[28:7:3] # Eightfold Path (Ashtangika Marga)
[28:7:4] TABLE head: Group | Components
  [28:7:4.0] Prajna (wisdom) | Right View (samma-ditthi), Right Intention (sankappa)
  [28:7:4.1] Sila (ethics) | Right Speech, Right Action, Right Livelihood
  [28:7:4.2] Samadhi (mental discipline) | Right Effort, Right Mindfulness, Right Concentration
[28:7:5] - Called the **Middle Path** because it **avoids self-indulgence as well as self-mortification** (APSC 2017).
[28:7:6] ~~ Sources: NextGen Indian History - APPSC Exams Part 1 p.63-64; PMF Ancient & Medieval Part 2 p.44-45; old app notes row 28 (sub 7, 8)
## SUB 8 | Symbols in Early Buddhist Art
[28:8:0] TABLE head: Event | Symbol
  [28:8:0.0] Birth | Lotus and bull
  [28:8:0.1] Great Renunciation | Horse
  [28:8:0.2] Meditation | Empty seat
  [28:8:0.3] Enlightenment | Bodhi (peepal) tree
  [28:8:0.4] First sermon | Wheel (Dharmachakra), often with deer (Deer Park)
  [28:8:0.5] Mahaparinirvana | Stupa
[28:8:1] - Early (aniconic) art showed the Buddha only through such symbols; **UPSC 2026:** the **empty seat = meditation** of the Buddha (not enlightenment, which is the tree).
[28:8:2] - **Wheel with deer** = the **first preaching** at Sarnath.
[28:8:3] ~~ Sources: PMF Ancient & Medieval Part 2 p.49 (UPSC 2026 key); NCERT Themes in Indian History I ch.4; old app notes row 28 (sub 4)
## SUB 9 | Pratityasamutpada – Dependent Origination
[28:9:0] - **Pratityasamutpada (Paticca-samuppada):** every phenomenon arises from a cause; a **12-link** chain (**12 nidanas**, bhavachakra); removing ignorance ends the chain.
[28:9:1] TABLE head: Order | Links
  [28:9:1.0] 1–4 | Avidya (ignorance) → Samskara (impressions) → Vijnana (consciousness) → Namarupa
  [28:9:1.1] 5–8 | Shadayatana (six senses) → Sparsha (contact) → Vedana (feeling) → Trishna (craving)
  [28:9:1.2] 9–12 | Upadana (clinging) → Bhava (becoming) → Jati (birth) → Jara-marana (old age–death)
[28:9:2] - **Cause of Samskara = Avidya** (CGPSC 2024) – the first link and root of suffering; Vijnana comes after Samskara.
[28:9:3] ~~ Sources: PMF Ancient & Medieval Part 2 p.45; old app notes row 28 (sub 9)
